<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the two-summand form of Beauville's compatible splitting conjecture. If the tangent bundle of a compact connected Kähler manifold decomposes into two integrable holomorphic subbundles of positive rank, then its ordinary universal cover admits a product decomposition whose factor tangent bundles are the lifted specified summands.

- **claim-compatible-universal-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a compact connected Kähler manifold of complex dimension $n\geq2$, and let $$T_X=E_1\oplus E_2$$ be a specified decomposition into integrable holomorphic subbundles of positive ranks $r_1,r_2$. If $\pi:\widetilde X\to X$ is the ordinary universal covering map, there are connected simply connected complex manifolds $Y_1,Y_2$ and a biholomorphism $\Phi:\widetilde X\to Y_1\times Y_2$ such that $$\mathrm d\Phi(\pi^*E_i)=\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_i^*T_{Y_i}\qquad(i=1,2),$$ where $\mathrm d\pi$ identifies $T_{\widetilde X}$ with $\pi^*T_X$. In particular, $\dim_\mathbb CY_i=r_i$.
  - Explanation: The ordinary universal cover splits into simply connected complex factors whose tangent bundles realize the supplied integrable summands.
- **claim-non-uniruled-integrability** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-non-uniruled)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected non-uniruled projective complex manifold, and let $T_X=E_1\oplus E_2$ be a specified holomorphic splitting into subbundles of positive ranks. Then both summands are integrable and the ordinary universal cover has a compatible product decomposition as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: In the non-uniruled projective setting, integrability follows automatically and the compatible universal-cover theorem applies.
- **claim-nef-big-compatible-product** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-nef-big)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex manifold of dimension $n\geq2$ with nef and big canonical bundle $K_X$. Every specified holomorphic splitting $T_X=E_1\oplus E_2$ into positive-rank subbundles is integrable and induces a compatible product decomposition of the ordinary universal cover as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: A nef and big canonical bundle guarantees integrability of every supplied positive-rank holomorphic splitting and hence a compatible universal-cover product.
- **claim-separated-product-charts** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-product-charts)): Every point of $X$ has a holomorphic coordinate neighborhood with coordinates $z=(z_1,z_2)$ and range $\mathbb D^{r_1}\times\mathbb D^{r_2}$, in which $E_i$ is tangent to the $z_i$-directions. Changes between these charts are locally of the following form, with each $h_i$ a local biholomorphism: $$\label{eq:separated-transition} z'_1=h_1(z_1),\qquad z'_2=h_2(z_2).$$
  - Explanation: Complementary integrable distributions admit simultaneous holomorphic product charts, and chart changes separate locally into their factor coordinates.
- **claim-hartogs-box-extension** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-hartogs)): Let $(s,t)$ be the standard coordinates of $\mathbb C^2$, and let $(w,v)$ be any invertible complex-affine coordinate system. For real numbers $0<e<a$ and $0<c<b$, put $$H=\bigl\{\|w\|<e,\ \|v\|<b\bigr\} \ \cup\ \bigl\{\|w\|<a,\ c<\|v\|<b\bigr\}, \qquad Q=\bigl\{\|w\|<a,\ \|v\|<b\bigr\}.$$ Every holomorphic map $f:H\to X$ satisfying $$\mathrm df(\partial_s)\in E_1, \qquad \mathrm df(\partial_t)\in E_2$$ extends uniquely to a holomorphic map $Q\to X$ with the same tangencies. Thus the affine coordinates defining the Hartogs figure need not be the coordinates defining the box.
  - Explanation: Separate tangencies remove the exceptional fibers of a meromorphic extension, giving unique holomorphic extension even when the affine Hartogs coordinates mix the prescribed parameter directions.
- **claim-small-area-derivative-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-small-area)): Let $M$ be a compact Hermitian manifold, with associated positive $(1,1)$-form $\omega_M$, and let $U\subset\mathbb C$ be a domain. Suppose that $h_j:U\to M$ are holomorphic and $$\int_U h_j^*\omega_M\longrightarrow 0.$$ Then $\left\lVert\mathrm dh_j\right\rVert$ tends to zero uniformly on every compact subset of $U$. Here the source has its Euclidean metric. Closedness of $\omega_M$ is not required.
  - Explanation: Vanishing area forces derivative norms to vanish uniformly on compact source subsets for a compact Hermitian target; closedness of the positive form is unnecessary.
- **claim-mixed-boundary-extension** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-mixed)): Let $B\subset\mathbb C^2_{s,t}$ be a convex open set, and let $f:B\to X$ be a holomorphic box. Suppose that $p\in\partial B$ has a $C^2$ defining function with nonzero differential. If the complex tangent line to $\partial B$ at $p$ is generated by a vector with both components nonzero, then $f$ extends as a holomorphic box to a neighborhood of $p$, agreeing with $f$ on its intersection with $B$.
  - Explanation: Convexity, a smooth defining boundary, and a tangent direction involving both parameter components allow continuation of the box across the boundary point.
- **claim-disk-axes-box** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-disk-axes)): Let $\alpha,\beta:\mathbb D\to X$ be holomorphic maps with $\mathrm d\alpha(T_\mathbb D)\subset E_1$, $\mathrm d\beta(T_\mathbb D)\subset E_2$, and $\alpha(0)=\beta(0)$. There is a unique holomorphic box $F:\mathbb D^2\to X$ such that $$F(s,0)=\alpha(s),\qquad F(0,t)=\beta(t).$$
  - Explanation: Holomorphic disk axes tangent to the respective summands and meeting at their origins determine a unique box on the entire bidisk.
- **claim-continuous-box-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-box-unique)): Two continuous boxes on $[0,1]^2$ with the same bottom and left edges are equal.
  - Explanation: A continuous box is determined by its bottom and left edges, using local separation propagated through a finite grid.
- **claim-polydisk-axes-box** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-polydisk-axes)): Let $S$ and $T$ be unit polydisks of positive dimensions. Suppose $\alpha:S\to X$ and $\beta:T\to X$ are holomorphic maps tangent to $E_1$ and $E_2$, respectively, and $\alpha(p_0)=\beta(q_0)$ for some $(p_0,q_0)\in S\times T$. There is a unique holomorphic box $F:S\times T\to X$ with $$F(p,q_0)=\alpha(p),\qquad F(p_0,q)=\beta(q).$$
  - Explanation: The disk construction extends to arbitrary positive-dimensional polydisk axes, with joint holomorphic parameter dependence and the prescribed tangencies.
- **claim-path-rectangles** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-rectangles)): The following assertions hold.* 1. *If $\alpha,\beta:[0,1]\to X$ locally follow $E_1$- and $E_2$-plaques, respectively, and $\alpha(0)=\beta(0)$, there is a continuous box $R:[0,1]^2\to X$ with $R(s,0)=\alpha(s)$ and $R(0,t)=\beta(t)$.* 2. *Every continuous path $\gamma:[0,1]\to X$ is the diagonal of a continuous box: $R(s,s)=\gamma(s)$.
  - Explanation: Plaque paths with a common start span a continuous box, and every continuous ambient path can serve as a box diagonal.
- **claim-intrinsic-leaf-manifolds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-leaves)): Each $L_i$ is a connected, Hausdorff, second-countable complex manifold of dimension $r_i$, and its inclusion into $X$ is an injective holomorphic immersion. It admits a connected simply connected cover $\widetilde L_i$, also a second-countable complex manifold. A path locally following $E_i$-plaques is continuous in the intrinsic topology of its leaf.
  - Explanation: Intrinsic plaque topology makes each leaf a suitable complex manifold for ordinary covering theory, and plaque paths remain continuous in that topology.
- **claim-inverse-germ-transport** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-germ-transport)): Let $R:[0,1]^2\to X$ be a continuous box with $R(0,0)=o$, and set $$\alpha(r)=R(r,0)\in L_1, \qquad \beta(r)=R(0,r)\in L_2, \qquad \gamma(r)=R(r,r)\in X.$$ There are mutually inverse local biholomorphism germs $H_r,K_r$ at $(\alpha(r),\beta(r))$ and $\gamma(r)$, respectively, which give analytic continuation of $H_o$ along $(\alpha,\beta)$ and of $K_o$ along $\gamma$.
  - Explanation: A continuous box transports local product germs and their inverses coherently along its axes and diagonal.
- **claim-crepant-canonical-contraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-canon-contraction)): There is a projective birational morphism with connected fibers $$f:X\longrightarrow Z$$ onto a normal projective variety with canonical singularities and ample $\mathbf Q$-Cartier canonical divisor. Moreover, $K_X=f^*K_Z$, and $f$ is an isomorphism over $Z_{\mathrm{reg}}$.
  - Explanation: The nef-and-big canonical regime supplies a connected-fiber birational canonical contraction that is crepant and unchanged over the smooth canonical locus.
- **claim-canonical-metric-completion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-canon-metric-input)): Fix a Kähler form $\omega_A$ from a polarization of $X$. There are smooth Kähler metrics $g_\varepsilon$ on $X$, for $0<\varepsilon<1$, whose forms satisfy $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(\omega_\varepsilon) =-\omega_\varepsilon+\varepsilon\omega_A, \qquad [\omega_\varepsilon] =2\pi c_1(K_X)+\varepsilon[\omega_A].$$ As $\varepsilon\to0$, they converge smoothly on compact subsets of $X^0$ to a Kähler metric $g$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g)=-g$. The completion of the intrinsic Riemannian distance of $(X^0,g)$ is naturally homeomorphic to $Z$.
  - Explanation: Canonical approximate Kähler–Einstein metrics converge smoothly on the regular canonical locus, and the limiting intrinsic distance completes to the canonical model.
- **claim-canonical-parallel-summands** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-canon-parallel)): The connection of $g$ preserves both $E_1\|_{X^0}$ and $E_2\|_{X^0}$. Both subbundles are integrable on all of $X$. They admit simultaneous local holomorphic product coordinates.
  - Explanation: Cancellation of Chern degrees forces the limiting second fundamental forms to vanish. Parallelism holds on the regular locus, and holomorphic Frobenius obstructions vanish everywhere.
- **claim-canonical-fiber-pseudodistance** (construction, [source](#stmt-canon-pseudodistance)): There is a real-analytic Kähler metric $h$ on $X^0$, uniformly equivalent to $g$, for which the given splitting is orthogonal and locally a Riemannian product. Its intrinsic distance completion is canonically homeomorphic to $Z$. Denoting the resulting distance on $Z$ by $\rho$, the function $$\label{canon:d} d(x,y)=\rho(f(x),f(y))$$ is a continuous pseudodistance on $X$, and $d(x,y)=0$ exactly when $f(x)=f(y)$.
  - Explanation: A parallel change of metric makes the summands orthogonal while preserving the completion. Pulling back the completed distance collapses precisely the canonical fibers.
- **claim-canonical-fiber-topology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-canon-fibers)): For every $z\in Z$, the reduced fiber $P=f^{-1}(z)_{\mathrm{red}}$ is a compact connected projective algebraic set. Any two points of $P$ are joined inside $P$ by a chain of images of rational curves, and $\pi_1(P)=1$ in the ordinary complex topology.
  - Explanation: Each reduced canonical fiber is projective and connected, is joined by rational chains, and is simply connected in the ordinary topology. The last property is supplied independently of rational-chain connectedness.
- **claim-coherent-ambient-box-germs** (construction, [source](#stmt-rat-germs)): Let $T$ be a rational tree or an interval, let $R:T\times T\to M$ be a box, and put $u(s)=R(s,s)$. There is a coherent family of holomorphic map germs $$c_{s,t}:(M\times M,(u(s),u(t)))\longrightarrow(M,R(s,t))$$ which agrees with ordinary crossing when $s=t$ and satisfies $$\label{rat:differential} \begin{aligned} \mathrm d_1c_{s,t}(T_M)&\subset E_1,&\qquad \mathrm d_1c_{s,t}(E_2)&=0,\\ \mathrm d_2c_{s,t}(T_M)&\subset E_2,&\qquad \mathrm d_2c_{s,t}(E_1)&=0. \end{aligned}$$ Here coherence means that near each parameter pair a single ambient holomorphic map represents all the nearby germs. This family is unique among coherent families agreeing with ordinary crossing on the diagonal.
  - Explanation: A box over a rational tree or interval determines coherent ambient holomorphic crossing germs, not merely crossing values or differential data.
- **claim-rational-surface-box-extension** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rat-extension)): Put $S=\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^1$. A holomorphic box on a neighborhood of the diagonal, or of the union $$D=(\mathbb P^1\times\{t_0\})\cup(\{s_0\}\times\mathbb P^1),$$ extends uniquely to a holomorphic box on $S$.
  - Explanation: A holomorphic box near the diagonal or the prescribed pair of axes extends uniquely across the product of projective lines.
- **claim-rational-tree-boxes** (construction, [source](#stmt-rat-boxes)): For every holomorphic map $u:T\to M$ from a rational tree there is a unique continuous box $$R_u:T\times T\longrightarrow M, \qquad R_u(s,s)=u(s),$$ holomorphic on every product of irreducible components. If a rational tree $T'$ contains $T$ as a subtree and $u':T'\to M$ extends $u$, then $R_{u'}\|_{T\times T}=R_u$. The ambient germ families of Lemma <a href="#rat:germs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rat:germs">21</a> agree on this restriction as well.
  - Explanation: A rational-tree map has a unique box extension on its square, holomorphic componentwise. Restriction to a subtree preserves both box values and ambient germ families.
- **claim-neighborhood-crossing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-crossing)): Let $P\subset M$ be a nonempty connected projective algebraic set. Suppose that $P$ is simply connected and joined by rational chains. There are a connected open neighborhood $U$ of $P$ and a holomorphic map $c:U\times U\to M$ that agrees near the diagonal with ordinary crossing in product charts. Throughout $U\times U$, it satisfies $$\label{cross:identities} \begin{gathered} c(x,x)=x,\qquad \mathrm d_1c(T_M)\subset E_1,\quad \mathrm d_1c(E_2)=0,\\ \mathrm d_2c(T_M)\subset E_2,\quad \mathrm d_2c(E_1)=0. \end{gathered}$$ The image subbundles in these formulas are evaluated at $c(x,y)$.
  - Explanation: A simply connected projective set joined by rational chains admits ordinary crossing on a neighborhood of its entire square, with the prescribed differential identities.
- **claim-tree-route-continuation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cross-tree-path)): Let $k:D\to Q$ be a holomorphic map from a rational tree with distinct smooth markings $m_1,m_2$. Every attained germ at $k(m_1)$ continues along the image of any path from $m_1$ to $m_2$. Its terminal germ is attained and is independent of the path in $D$.
  - Explanation: An attained germ continues along rational-tree routes, remains attained at the endpoint, and is independent of the route in the parameter tree.
- **claim-continuation-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cross-stability)): Suppose that a germ continues along a continuous path $\gamma:[0,1]\to N$ in a complex manifold $N$. There are a representative of its terminal germ and a uniform neighborhood of $\gamma$ in the space of paths starting at $\gamma(0)$ such that continuation along every path in that neighborhood exists and ends in that representative.
  - Explanation: A finite chain of ambient representatives also continues the initial germ along sufficiently close paths, with a shared terminal representative.
- **claim-compact-rational-route-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-cross-parameter-space)): There is a compact semialgebraic space $S_*$ of three-pointed rational-tree maps into $Q$ whose first evaluation is $q_*$ and whose second evaluation $$e:S_*\longrightarrow Q$$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: A compact semialgebraic family of marked rational-tree maps supplies a route from the fixed diagonal point to every point of the product set.
- **claim-nearby-degenerate-tree-routes** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cross-nearby-routes)): Fix a stable-map parameter in $S_*$ and a path between its first two markings, chosen along smooth component arcs and through the intervening nodes. For every sufficiently nearby parameter one can choose a path between its corresponding markings whose image in $Q$, after parametrization on $[0,1]$, is uniformly as close as desired to the fixed image path.
  - Explanation: Routes through a limiting nodal tree can be approximated uniformly in the target by routes in nearby stable-map fibers, including degenerations.
- **claim-semialgebraic-path-lifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-cross-semialgebraic-lift)): Every continuous semialgebraic path $\sigma:[0,1]\to Q$ has a finite subdivision such that on each closed subinterval it admits a continuous lift to $S_*$. Lifts on consecutive subintervals need not agree at their common endpoint.
  - Explanation: Every semialgebraic path has continuous lifts on finitely many closed subintervals. The lifts are not required to match at subdivision endpoints.
- **claim-attained-all-path-continuation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cross-all-paths)): Every attained germ continues along every continuous semialgebraic path in $Q$ starting at its basepoint, and the terminal germ is attained.
  - Explanation: Attained germs continue along every continuous semialgebraic path in the product set, and their terminal germs remain attained.
- **claim-crossing-separate-nonexpansion** (bound, [source](#stmt-global-nonexpansion)): For each fiber value $z\in Z$, one can choose a crossing map as above and $r>0$ such that $f^{-1}(B_{\rho}(z,r))\subset U$ and $$\label{global:lipschitz} \begin{split} d\bigl(c(a,b),c(a',b)\bigr)&\leq d(a,a'),\\ d\bigl(c(a,b),c(a,b')\bigr)&\leq d(b,b') \end{split}$$ whenever all the inputs in the relevant inequality belong to $f^{-1}(B_{\rho}(z,r/10))$.
  - Explanation: Near each canonical fiber, crossing is nonexpanding in either input separately for the continuous fiber-collapsing pseudodistance.
- **claim-collapsed-fiber-path-boxes** (construction, [source](#stmt-global-boxes)): The following boxes exist.* 1. *For every continuous path $\lambda\colon[0,1]\to X$, there is a box $R\colon[0,1]^2\to X$ such that $R(s,s)=\lambda(s)$.* 2. *For continuous paths $\alpha$ and $\beta$ in leaves of the first and second foliations, respectively, with $\alpha(0)=\beta(0)$, there is a box on $[0,1]^2$ satisfying $R(s,0)=\alpha(s)$ and $R(0,t)=\beta(t)$.* *Here continuity of the prescribed leaf paths is in the intrinsic leaf topology.
  - Explanation: Separate nonexpansion propagates tile diameter control, producing boxes for arbitrary diagonals and prescribed intrinsic leaf axes, including paths contained in collapsed fibers.
- **claim-canonical-alternative-product** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-global-product)): Under the hypotheses of Corollary <a href="#thm:nef-big" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:nef-big">3</a>, let $Y\to X$ be the universal cover and $\widetilde L_i\to L_i$ the universal covers of the leaves through $o$. Then $$Y\simeq\widetilde L_1\times\widetilde L_2$$ biholomorphically, with the lifts of $E_i$ identified with the respective tangent bundles of the factors.
  - Explanation: The collapsed-fiber continuation route yields inverse lifted maps between the universal cover and the product of intrinsic leaf universal covers, preserving the supplied summands.

        "exact_quote": "Fix a Kähler form $\\omega_A$ from a polarization of $X$. The completion of the intrinsic Riemannian distance of $(X^0,g)$ is naturally homeomorphic to $Z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-052.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There is a real-analytic Kähler metric $h$ on $X^0$, uniformly equivalent to $g$, for which the given splitting is orthogonal and locally a Riemannian product.         "anchor": "stmt-rat-germs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a rational tree or an interval, let $R:T\\times T\\to M$ be a box, and put $u(s)=R(s,s)$.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-rat-boxes",
        "exact_quote": "For every holomorphic map $u:T\\to M$ from a rational tree there is a unique continuous box $$R_u:T\\times T\\longrightarrow M,\n\\qquad R_u(s,s)=u(s),$$ holomorphic on every product of irreducible components.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-crossing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P\\subset M$ be a nonempty connected projective algebraic set. Suppose that $P$ is simply connected and joined by rational chains. There are a connected open neighborhood $U$ of $P$ and a holomorphic map $c:U\\times U\\to M$ that agrees near the diagonal with ordinary crossing in product charts. Throughout $U\\times U$, it satisfies $$\\label{cross:identities}\n\\begin{gathered}\n c(x,x)=x,\\qquad\n \\mathrm d_1c(T_M)\\subset E_1,\\quad \\mathrm d_1c(E_2)=0,\\\\\n \\mathrm d_2c(T_M)\\subset E_2,\\quad \\mathrm d_2c(E_1)=0.\n\\end{gathered}$$ The image subbundles in these formulas are evaluated at $c(x,y)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-052.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-crossing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P\\subset M$ be a nonempty connected projective algebraic set. Suppose that $P$ is simply connected and joined by rational chains. Throughout $U\\times U$, it satisfies $$\\label{cross:identities}\n\\begin{gathered}\n c(x,x)=x,\\qquad\n \\mathrm d_1c(T_M)\\subset E_1,\\quad \\mathrm d_1c(E_2)=0,\\\\\n \\mathrm d_2c(T_M)\\subset E_2,\\quad \\mathrm d_2c(E_1)=0.\n\\end{gathered}$$ The image subbundles in these formulas are evaluated at $c(x,y)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-052.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $P\\subset M$ be a nonempty connected projective algebraic set. Suppose that $P$ is simply connected and joined by rational chains. Throughout $U\\times U$, it satisfies $$\\label{cross:identities}\n\\begin{gathered}\n c(x,x)=x,\\qquad\n \\mathrm d_1c(T_M)\\subset E_1,\\quad \\mathrm d_1c(E_2)=0,\\\\\n \\mathrm d_2c(T_M)\\subset E_2,\\quad \\mathrm d_2c(E_1)=0.\n\\end{gathered}$$ The image subbundles in these formulas are evaluated at $c(x,y)$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Integrable holomorphic subbundle** ([source](#sec:intro)): A subbundle is *integrable* if its local holomorphic sections are closed under the Lie bracket.
- **Holomorphic box** ([source](#stmt-def-box)): A holomorphic map $f:U\to X$ is a *holomorphic box* if $$\mathrm df(T\mathbb C^a\oplus0)\subset E_1,\qquad \mathrm df(0\oplus T\mathbb C^b)\subset E_2.$$
- **Nef and big line bundle** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a smooth projective manifold, a line bundle is *nef* if it has nonnegative degree on every curve, and it is *big* if its sections have maximal asymptotic growth.
- **Regular canonical locus** ([source](#pm-h-4)): We call $X^0=f^{-1}(Z_{\mathrm{reg}})$, the inverse image of the smooth locus of $Z$, the *regular canonical locus*.
- **Box on topological parameter spaces** ([source](#stmt-rat-definition)): A continuous map $R:A\times B\to M$ is a *box* if, near every parameter pair, there are a product neighborhood $A'\times B'$ and a product chart on its image in which $$z\bigl(R(a,b)\bigr)=\bigl(r_1(a),r_2(b)\bigr).$$
- **Rational tree** ([source](#stmt-rat-definition)): A *rational tree* is a connected nodal curve whose irreducible components are copies of $\mathbb P^1$ and whose dual graph is a finite tree.
- **Ordinary local crossing** ([source](#rat:local-crossing)): Consequently the local crossing $$\label{rat:local-crossing} \kappa(x,y)=z^{-1}\bigl(z_1(x),z_2(y)\bigr)$$ is independent, as a germ along the diagonal, of the product chart.
- **Coherent ambient germ family** ([source](#stmt-rat-germs)): Here coherence means that near each parameter pair a single ambient holomorphic map represents all the nearby germs.
- **Attained crossing germ** ([source](#stmt-cross-attained)): A germ over $(a,b)\in Q$ is *attained* if it is the crossing germ of Lemma <a href="#rat:germs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rat:germs">21</a> associated to a rational-tree box $R_u$ of Proposition <a href="#rat:boxes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rat:boxes">24</a>, at two distinct smooth marked points $s,t$ of its parameter tree, with $u(s)=a$ and $u(t)=b$.
- **Joined by rational chains** ([source](#app:crossing)): A set is *joined by rational chains* if every two of its points can be joined inside it by a finite chain of images of $\mathbb P^1$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-compact-projection-bound | \sup_{x\in X}\left\lVert P_i(x)\right\rVert | < | $\infty$ | The supplied complex-linear projections. |
| q-boundary-slice-radius | r(x) | = | $k\sqrt{-x}$ | Negative real normal coordinates sufficiently close to the boundary. |
| q-boundary-normal-speed | \left\lVert\mathrm df(e_u)\right\rVert | ≤ | $C\left\lVert\mathrm df(e_v)\right\rVert$ | The fixed affine normal and complex tangent directions. |
| q-boundary-area-upper-bound | A(x) | ≤ | $A(x_0)$ | $x_0\le x<0$ |
| q-boundary-distance-shell-ratio | \frac{\delta_j}{\lambda_j} | ≤ | $\delta_j^{1/4}$ | The chosen intermediate scales. |
| q-shell-slice-radius-ratio | \frac{\lambda_j}{\sqrt{\delta_j}} | ≤ | $\delta_j^{1/8}$ | The same chosen small-area annulus sequence. |
| q-canonical-energy-decay | Integrated squared second fundamental forms | = | $O(\varepsilon)$ | $\varepsilon\to0$ |
| q-limiting-canonical-ricci | \mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g) | = | $-g$ | Smooth convergence on compact subsets of $X^0$. |
| q-canonical-fiber-fundamental-group | \pi_1(P) | = | $1$ | Every canonical fiber value. |
| q-crossing-first-variable-nonexpansion | d\bigl(c(a,b),c(a',b)\bigr) | ≤ | $d(a,a')$ | A crossing domain and radius chosen for the fiber value. |
| q-crossing-second-variable-nonexpansion | d\bigl(c(a,b),c(a,b')\bigr) | ≤ | $d(b,b')$ | All inputs in the stipulated inverse-image ball. |
| q-uniform-crossing-diameter | \operatorname{diam}_d(S) | ≤ | $2\delta$ | Subsets of the compact manifold, measured by its canonical pseudodistance. |

Closedness of $\\Omega$ and Stokes’ theorem, applied to the moving disks, yield $$A'(x)=\\int_0^{2\\pi}\n   \\Omega\\bigl(e_u+r'(x)\\partial_r,\\partial_\\theta\\bigr)\n       \\big|_{u=x,\\ v=r(x)e^{i\\theta}}\\,\\mathrm d\\theta.$$ Indeed the boundary velocity is $e_u+r'(x)\\partial_r$, and the circles have their counterclockwise orientation. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-product-charts** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-charts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-charts): Combine Frobenius submersions for the complementary distributions and apply the holomorphic inverse function theorem. -> Simultaneous product charts with locally separated transitions.
- **step-hartogs-extension** [claim](#stmt-prop-hartogs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hartogs): Extend meromorphically, exclude exceptional curves by incompatible analytic level dimensions, and remove the remaining singleton graph fibers. -> Unique holomorphic box on the filled bidisk.
- **step-small-area** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-area) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-area): Normalize a putatively nonvanishing derivative and use compactness and holomorphic convergence to contradict vanishing area. -> Uniform derivative decay on compact source subsets.
- **step-mixed-boundary** [claim](#stmt-prop-mixed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-mixed): Bound shrinking slice areas by Stokes, select a small-area annulus, construct a transverse shell in a product chart, and fill it. -> Holomorphic box extension across the boundary point.
- **step-disk-axes** [claim](#stmt-prop-disk-axes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-disk-axes): Continue a box germ through convex domains; extend mixed boundary points and treat axis boundary points in product charts. -> Unique full-bidisk box.
- **step-box-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-lem-box-unique) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-box-unique): Propagate equality across a finite grid whose tile images fit in product charts. -> Equality of the boxes.
- **step-polydisk-axes** [claim](#stmt-prop-polydisk-axes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-polydisk-axes): Build pointwise radial disk boxes, reconstruct nearby parameter squares in fixed charts, and use uniqueness to prove joint holomorphicity. -> Unique holomorphic product box retaining both parameter tangencies.
- **step-path-rectangles** [claim](#stmt-prop-rectangles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rectangles): Fill finite grids while preserving whole-polydisk representatives of transported edges; use diagonal tiles for arbitrary ambient paths. -> Continuous boxes with the required axes or diagonal.
- **step-intrinsic-leaves** [claim](#stmt-lem-leaves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-leaves): Use plaque chains and a countable ambient atlas to construct intrinsic leaf manifolds and their ordinary universal covers. -> Hausdorff second-countable leaf manifolds and simply connected covers.
- **step-inverse-germs** [claim](#stmt-prop-germ-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-germ-transport): Compose separated chart transitions along box slices and keep fixed chains near each parameter to obtain actual analytic continuation. -> Mutually inverse continued local biholomorphism germs.
- **step-main-product** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-germ-transport-2): Continue both inverse germs along all paths, apply monodromy on simply connected covers, lift with matched basepoints, and extend the inverse and tangent identities holomorphically. -> Compatible biholomorphism from the ordinary universal cover to the product of leaf covers.
- **step-non-uniruled** [claim](#stmt-cor-non-uniruled) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-non-uniruled): Apply the cited automatic integrability theorem and then the compact Kähler splitting theorem. -> Integrability and compatible universal-cover product.
- **step-canonical-contraction** [claim](#stmt-canon-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-canon-contraction): Apply base-point freeness and Stein factorization, identify the canonical divisor by reflexive extension, and prove crepancy and regular-locus isomorphism. -> Crepant canonical contraction with connected fibers.
- **step-canonical-metric** [claim](#stmt-canon-metric-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-canon-metric-input): Apply the cited canonical metric approximation and metric-completion results to the crepant resolution. -> Approximate Kähler–Einstein metrics, smooth regular-locus limit, and completion identified with the canonical model.
- **step-canonical-parallelism** [claim](#stmt-canon-parallel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-canon-parallel): Sum subbundle curvature identities, cancel total Chern degrees, force limiting second fundamental forms to vanish, and extend Frobenius-obstruction vanishing. -> Parallelism on the regular canonical locus and integrability everywhere.
- **step-nef-big-product** [claim](#stmt-thm-nef-big) / [proof](#proof-stmt-canon-parallel-2): Combine the established integrability with the compact Kähler splitting theorem. -> Compatible ordinary universal-cover product.
- **step-canonical-pseudodistance** [claim](#stmt-canon-pseudodistance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-canon-pseudodistance): Orthogonalize by parallel projections, use constant eigenvalues for uniform metric equivalence, and pull back the completed distance. -> Real-analytic local product metric and continuous pseudodistance collapsing exactly the fibers.
- **step-canonical-fibers** [claim](#stmt-canon-fibers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-canon-fibers): Apply the cited resolution-fiber rational-chain theorem and the separate ordinary simple-connectedness theorem. -> Compact connected projective fibers with rational chains and trivial ordinary fundamental group.
- **step-box-ambient-germs** [claim](#stmt-rat-germs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rat-germs): Combine transverse holonomy along box slices and prove coherence by fixed finite chart chains. -> Unique coherent family of ambient crossing germs.
- **step-rational-extension** [claim](#stmt-rat-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rat-extension): Extend projective coordinate ratios across a Stein complement, resolve indeterminacies, contract exceptional images by complementary tangency, and descend. -> Holomorphic box on the product of projective lines.
- **step-rational-tree-boxes** [claim](#stmt-rat-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rat-boxes): Extend component squares and fill component pairs in increasing tree distance, matching inward axes and ambient germs. -> Unique componentwise holomorphic continuous box compatible with subtree restriction.
- **step-tree-route-continuation** [claim](#stmt-cross-tree-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-tree-path): Attach separate route trees to the realizing markings and use subtree compatibility of boxes and germs. -> Path-independent attained terminal germ along the tree route.
- **step-continuation-stability** [claim](#stmt-cross-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-stability): Shrink a finite continuation chain so uniformly nearby paths traverse the same representative domains and equality overlaps. -> Stable continuation with a shared terminal representative.
- **step-compact-route-family** [claim](#stmt-cross-parameter-space) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-parameter-space): Use proper fixed-degree stable-map spaces and the Baire category theorem to select a finite collection of degrees covering all irreducible components. -> Compact semialgebraic route parameter space with surjective evaluation.
- **step-nearby-routes** [claim](#stmt-cross-nearby-routes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-nearby-routes): Move smooth arcs in relative charts and join them through small nodal plumbing neighborhoods. -> Uniformly close image routes for nearby parameters.
- **step-semialgebraic-lifts** [claim](#stmt-cross-semialgebraic-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-semialgebraic-lift): Apply semialgebraic choice, divide into continuity intervals, and extend lifts to their endpoints using compactness and one-sided limits. -> Continuous lifts on finitely many closed subintervals.
- **step-attained-all-paths** [claim](#stmt-cross-all-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-all-paths): On each lifted interval fix a germ at the common route origin, transport through its trees, and combine nearby routes with continuation stability. -> Continuation along the whole path with attained terminal germ.
- **step-neighborhood-crossing** [claim](#stmt-thm-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-all-paths-2): Remove monodromy through a semialgebraic triangulation and simple connectedness, glue coherent ambient germs near the compact product set, and restrict to a neighborhood square. -> Holomorphic neighborhood crossing with diagonal and differential identities.
- **step-separate-nonexpansion** [claim](#stmt-global-nonexpansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-global-nonexpansion): Extend local product tensor identities by real analyticity, perturb regular-locus paths away from analytic exceptional sets, and pass to all inputs by continuity. -> Separate nonexpansion for the fiber-collapsing pseudodistance.
- **step-collapsed-grid-boxes** [claim](#stmt-global-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-global-boxes): Choose a uniform crossing scale by compactness and fill finite grids while preserving partial-slice diameter bounds. -> Continuous boxes with the prescribed diagonal or axes, also inside collapsed fibers.
- **step-alternative-cover-product** [claim](#stmt-global-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-global-product): Use the alternative boxes for inverse-germ transport, apply monodromy and matched covering lifts, and extend inverse and tangent identities. -> Compatible product of intrinsic leaf universal covers.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-compatible-universal-cover**: The ordinary universal cover splits biholomorphically into simply connected complex factors whose tangent bundles realize the lifted specified summands.
- **invariant-box-tangencies**: A holomorphic box respects the prescribed parameter directions without requiring immersion or full rank.
- **invariant-box-uniqueness**: Matching bottom and left edges determine a continuous box uniquely.
- **invariant-canonical-parallelism**: The limiting canonical metric connection preserves the supplied summands on the regular canonical locus, and the summands are integrable everywhere.
- **invariant-fiber-pseudodistance**: The canonical-model distance pulls back to a continuous pseudodistance whose zero-distance classes are exactly the contraction fibers.
- **invariant-ambient-crossing**: A simply connected projective set joined by rational chains has an ambient neighborhood crossing map that fixes the diagonal and respects the specified distributions in each input.
- **invariant-separate-nonexpansion**: Near each canonical fiber, crossing is nonexpanding in either input separately when every input remains in the prescribed smaller inverse-image ball.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-unverified-integrability**: Infer a compatible product from an arbitrary holomorphic tangent splitting. -> A specified complementary summand can fail to be integrable even when the ambient manifold is already a product.
- **anti-use-missing-kahler-compactness**: Apply the global splitting conclusion to a noncompact complex manifold or to a compact Hermitian manifold without the Kähler hypothesis. -> The stated global theorem does not cover that ambient regime; its extension argument uses compactness and closedness of the Kähler form.
- **anti-use-finite-cover-product**: Replace the ordinary universal-cover conclusion by a product decomposition after a finite cover. -> The asserted covering-space product does not supply the stronger finite-cover conclusion.
- **anti-use-pseudodistance-as-distance**: Treat zero canonical pseudodistance as equality of points in the original manifold. -> The pseudodistance identifies points throughout each canonical fiber.
- **anti-use-rational-chains-imply-simple-connectivity**: Omit simple connectedness from the neighborhood crossing theorem because rational chains already connect the projective set. -> Rational routes supply continuation but do not by themselves justify removal of monodromy for a possibly singular set.
- **anti-use-values-determine-ambient-germs**: Identify ambient crossing germs solely from their values and differential tangencies. -> Different parameter pairs with the same ambient input have not yet been shown to yield the same germ before the monodromy argument.
- **anti-use-unrestricted-nonexpansion**: Apply separate nonexpansion to arbitrary inputs throughout the manifold. -> The lemma supplies a local estimate only when every relevant input lies in the designated smaller inverse-image ball.
- **anti-use-pointwise-boxes-imply-holomorphic-family**: Infer joint holomorphic dependence merely from the existence of a box for each parameter pair. -> Pointwise box existence alone does not establish parameter continuity or holomorphicity.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results concern holomorphic maps on manifolds, arbitrary continuous paths, and universal covers. Their finite grid arguments depend on analytically supplied charts and crossing maps; they do not specify a finite executable input-output construction. The universal-cover proof explicitly invokes monodromy: “The monodromy theorem, applied on these simply connected source covers, gives holomorphic maps $$H:Y_1\times Y_2\longrightarrow X, \qquad K:\widetilde X\longrightarrow L_1\times L_2$$ with the prescribed initial germs.” A finite numerical or combinatorial test would not decide these global analytic conclusions.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compatible universal-cover decomposition supplies a geometric existence result, without a finite representation of its factors or an executable software interface. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Continuous path rectangles establish geometric continuation; they do not specify a simulation update rule, discretization, or numerical error control. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The neighborhood-crossing theorem asserts existence of an open domain and a holomorphic map, without a method for encoding that domain or evaluating the map in a computational tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact family of rational routes is obtained using Baire category to select degrees. This argument does not provide a degree-selection algorithm or a computational stopping criterion. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Rational trees organize componentwise geometric extension, but their components carry holomorphic maps and ambient germs. The paper does not provide a finite data representation or operations for these analytic objects. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The second fundamental form estimate concerns convergence of geometric energy under Kähler–Einstein approximation. It specifies no hardware workload, arithmetic representation, or accelerator construction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Simple connectedness and rational-chain connectivity of canonical fibers supply topological inputs for continuation. They establish no cryptographic primitive, adversarial model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The small-area lemma controls derivatives of holomorphic maps into a compact Hermitian manifold. It supplies neither a learning construction nor a guarantee for arbitrary trained maps. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Separate nonexpansion is proved for holomorphic crossing maps in the canonical pseudodistance. The result supplies no objective function, optimization iteration, or convergence criterion. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Coherence of ambient germs is local over a geometric parameter space. It does not establish agreement between distinct parameter pairs with the same ambient input, much less a distributed consistency protocol. |
| other (Complex geometry and holomorphic foliations) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | Product charts construct local crossing by combining the first coordinate of the first input with the second coordinate of the second input. For a simply connected projective set joined by rational chains, the neighborhood-crossing theorem extends this operation to a neighborhood of the whole set. This is an abstract geometric construction; computational evaluation is not supplied. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-compact-kahler-ambient** (hypothesis): The main compatible splitting theorem requires a compact connected Kähler manifold; it does not establish the same conclusion for arbitrary noncompact or merely Hermitian manifolds. Blocked use: ['Applying the global product conclusion without verifying compactness, connectedness, and the Kähler hypothesis.']
- **limit-specified-integrable-pair** (hypothesis): Both specified complementary holomorphic tangent summands must be integrable. An existing product structure does not make an arbitrary supplied splitting compatible. Blocked use: ['Inferring compatibility solely from a holomorphic direct-sum decomposition.', 'Replacing the supplied summands by unrelated factor directions.']
- **limit-universal-cover-conclusion** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns the ordinary universal cover. It does not assert a product decomposition after a finite cover, and its factors need not be compact. Blocked use: ['Claiming that a finite cover is a product.', 'Assuming compactness of the intrinsic leaves or their universal covers.']
- **limit-local-separated-transitions** (formal_scope): Separated chart transitions are local assertions. They do not supply a single separated formula across a disconnected overlap. Blocked use: ['Using a single block-coordinate transformation on every component of an overlap without checking its domain.']
- **limit-box-tangency-without-rank** (formal_scope): A holomorphic box is defined by the prescribed parameter tangencies and need not be an immersion. Its existence supplies no full-rank parametrization of the target. Blocked use: ['Treating every box as a coordinate chart or an immersed product.', 'Changing the prescribed parameter directions when using affine coordinates for its source domain.']
- **limit-hartogs-adapted-extension** (hypothesis): The Hartogs extension proposition applies to maps satisfying both prescribed tangencies and to the stipulated Hartogs figure in the compact Kähler target. Its removal of exceptional fibers is specific to these box conditions. Blocked use: ['Claiming holomorphic removal of exceptional fibers for an arbitrary meromorphic map.', 'Using a source shell without establishing the stated figure and tangencies.']
- **limit-small-area-qualitative-regime** (approximation): Small-area derivative decay concerns holomorphic maps into a compact Hermitian target and convergence on compact source subsets. It gives no stated numerical modulus, and closedness of the Hermitian form is unnecessary for this lemma. Blocked use: ['Inferring uniform derivative decay up to an uncontrolled source boundary.', 'Treating small sampled area as a certified quantitative derivative bound.', "Importing the lemma's lack of a closedness requirement into the global Kähler extension argument."]
- **limit-mixed-boundary-conditions** (hypothesis): Mixed-boundary extension requires a convex source, the stated regular defining function, and a complex tangent direction with both components nonzero. The proof treats the axis boundary points separately when assembling disk boxes. Blocked use: ['Applying mixed-boundary extension at an axis tangent or an irregular boundary point.', 'Dropping convexity from the proposition without another continuation argument.']
- **limit-holomorphic-parameter-dependence** (formal_scope): Pointwise existence of radial boxes does not itself establish continuity or holomorphic dependence on additional parameters. The proof separately reconstructs nearby squares using fixed product charts. Blocked use: ['Asserting a jointly holomorphic family merely because a box exists for each parameter pair.', 'Shrinking transported edge domains and then assuming the full-polydisk induction remains valid.']
- **limit-intrinsic-leaf-topology** (formal_scope): The leaf factors use intrinsic plaque topology. Ambient subspace continuity alone is not a substitute for the intrinsic continuity required of prescribed leaf paths. Blocked use: ['Constructing leaf covers from the ambient subspace topology without justification.', 'Feeding merely ambient-continuous leaf-valued paths into the prescribed-axis argument.']
- **limit-global-continuation-not-finite-extraction** (nonconstructive): The global factorization is obtained through all-path continuation, monodromy, and covering lifts. These establish a biholomorphism without specifying a finite computational representation or a factor-extraction procedure. Blocked use: ['Advertising the theorem as an executable decomposition algorithm.', 'Assigning a runtime or a numerical accuracy guarantee to the universal-cover construction.']
- **limit-projective-automatic-integrability** (hypothesis): Automatic integrability is supplied under the non-uniruled projective hypotheses or the nef-and-big canonical hypotheses. These corollaries do not remove integrability from the main theorem for arbitrary compact Kähler manifolds. Blocked use: ['Assuming every holomorphic tangent splitting on every compact Kähler manifold is integrable.', 'Applying the canonical contraction or metric argument without the projective positivity assumptions.']
- **limit-canonical-metric-regular-locus** (approximation): Smooth convergence of the approximate canonical metrics and parallelism of the limiting connection are asserted on the regular canonical locus. Integrability extends over the whole manifold, but this does not extend the smooth limiting metric there. Blocked use: ['Claiming smooth metric convergence across every exceptional fiber.', 'Claiming that the limiting canonical connection is defined smoothly on all of the original manifold.']
- **limit-pseudodistance-separates-fibers** (edge_case): The pulled-back canonical distance is a pseudodistance. Distinct points in a contraction fiber have zero pseudodistance, so diameter control does not imply ordinary target-space size control. Blocked use: ['Inferring equality of points from zero pseudodistance.', 'Replacing pseudodiameter bounds by ordinary diameter bounds.', 'Assigning finite limiting-metric length to every path in a collapsed fiber.']
- **limit-rational-box-ambient-regime** (hypothesis): The rational-box extension arguments use a smooth projective target with complementary integrable tangent subbundles. Their rational-tree construction includes component maps, nodal compatibility, and ambient germ families, rather than merely an abstract finite tree. Blocked use: ['Extending the rational-box theorem to arbitrary singular targets.', 'Treating a combinatorial tree alone as sufficient input for evaluating a rational box.']
- **limit-neighborhood-crossing-topology** (hypothesis): Neighborhood crossing requires a nonempty connected projective algebraic set that is both simply connected and joined by rational chains. Rational chains provide routes; simple connectedness separately removes monodromy. Blocked use: ['Dropping simple connectedness because rational chains connect the set.', 'Inferring ordinary simple connectedness of a possibly singular set from rational-chain connectivity alone.']
- **limit-coherence-not-repeated-input-identification** (formal_scope): Local coherence over the parameter space does not yet identify germs obtained at distinct parameter pairs with the same ambient input. Values and differential tangencies alone do not specify the ambient germ. Blocked use: ['Gluing ambient maps using only equality of their values on the projective set.', 'Skipping the monodromy argument when repeated ambient inputs occur.']
- **limit-rational-route-degree-selection** (nonconstructive): The compact rational-route family is obtained by an existential degree selection using Baire category. The proof states no effective degree bound for searching for such a family. Blocked use: ['Claiming an effective stopping criterion for enumeration of rational routes.', 'Interpreting existence of the compact route family as a computed representation of it.']
- **limit-piecewise-semialgebraic-lifts** (formal_scope): The path-lifting lemma provides continuous lifts separately on closed subintervals of a semialgebraic path. Consecutive lifts need not match, and continuation across their junctions uses the attained-germ argument. Blocked use: ['Assuming a single continuous lift over the whole path.', 'Applying the semialgebraic lifting lemma directly to an arbitrary continuous path without the later continuation argument.']
- **limit-local-separate-nonexpansion** (hypothesis): Separate nonexpansion is local to the selected crossing neighborhood and requires all relevant inputs to lie in the stipulated smaller inverse-image ball. It is not asserted for arbitrary inputs throughout the manifold. Blocked use: ['Applying the inequalities outside their local input regime.', 'Treating separate nonexpansion as a strict contraction or a convergence theorem for an iteration.']
- **limit-finite-grid-supplied-analytic-data** (nonconstructive): The finite grid constructions use product charts, holomorphic boxes, or crossing maps already supplied by the analytic arguments. Finite tiling alone does not turn these constructions into an effective procedure from unspecified manifold data. Blocked use: ['Certifying the global analytic theorem by testing only a finite coordinate example.', 'Claiming computational complexity from the finiteness of a chosen grid.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 052 (`lean/docs/052.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Universal-cover splitting for compact Kähler manifolds](../../preprints/Universal-cover-splitting-for-compact-Kahler-manifolds-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Integrability of split tangent bundles on rationally connected manifolds](../../preprints/Integrability-of-split-tangent-bundles-on-rationally-connected-manifolds-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The splitting question asks whether a holomorphic decomposition of the tangent bundle comes from a product decomposition of the universal cover. The formalized result gives an affirmative answer for a compact connected Kähler manifold whose tangent bundle splits into two positive-rank, integrable holomorphic subbundles. The universal cover is a product of connected simply connected complex manifolds of the prescribed dimensions, and the differential identifies the two tangent factors with the original summands.
>
> Both integrability assumptions are required. Automatic integrability and the paper's additional corollaries are not included.
>
> The formalization proves integrability of both summands in a holomorphic splitting of the tangent bundle of a rationally connected projective manifold. More precisely, for a compact connected complex manifold of dimension at least two with a projective embedding witnessing rational connectedness, each positive-rank summand of a holomorphic tangent-bundle splitting is integrable. The paper's compatible product decomposition is a separate consequence, outside this selected statement.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open scoped Manifold ContDiff
open Set

namespace UniversalCoverSplitting

universe u v

abbrev Model (n : ℕ) := Fin n → ℂ
abbrev model (n : ℕ) := 𝓘(ℂ, Model n)

structure ComplexManifold (n : ℕ) where
  Carrier : Type u
  [topology : TopologicalSpace Carrier]
  [charted : ChartedSpace (Model n) Carrier]
  [hausdorff : T2Space Carrier]
  [secondCountable : SecondCountableTopology Carrier]
  [complexSmooth : IsManifold (model n) ∞ Carrier]

attribute [instance] ComplexManifold.topology ComplexManifold.charted
  ComplexManifold.hausdorff ComplexManifold.secondCountable ComplexManifold.complexSmooth

variable {n r₁ r₂ : ℕ}

abbrev Tangent (X : ComplexManifold.{u} n) (x : X.Carrier) := TangentSpace (model n) x
abbrev RealMetricField (X : ComplexManifold.{u} n) :=
  ∀ x : X.Carrier, Tangent X x →ₗ[ℝ] Tangent X x →ₗ[ℝ] ℝ

def metricInChart {X : ComplexManifold.{u} n} (g : RealMetricField X)
    (c : X.Carrier) (z u v : Model n) : ℝ :=
  let e := extChartAt (model n) c
  let q := mfderiv (model n) (model n) e.symm z
  g (e.symm z) (q u) (q v)

def fundamentalInChart {X : ComplexManifold.{u} n} (g : RealMetricField X)
    (c : X.Carrier) (z u v : Model n) : ℝ :=
  metricInChart g c z (Complex.I • u) v

structure KahlerMetric (X : ComplexManifold.{u} n) where
  metric : RealMetricField X
  symmetric : ∀ x u v, metric x u v = metric x v u
  positive : ∀ x u, u ≠ 0 → 0 < metric x u u
  hermitian : ∀ x u v, metric x (Complex.I • u) (Complex.I • v) = metric x u v
  smooth : ∀ c u v, ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (fun z ↦ metricInChart metric c z u v)
    (extChartAt (model n) c).target
  closed : ∀ c z, z ∈ (extChartAt (model n) c).target → ∀ u v w : Model n,
    fderiv ℝ (fun y ↦ fundamentalInChart metric c y v w) z u +
    fderiv ℝ (fun y ↦ fundamentalInChart metric c y w u) z v +
    fderiv ℝ (fun y ↦ fundamentalInChart metric c y u v) z w = 0

abbrev ProjectionField (X : ComplexManifold.{u} n) :=
  ∀ x : X.Carrier, Tangent X x →L[ℂ] Tangent X x

def projectionInChart {X : ComplexManifold.{u} n} (P : ProjectionField X)
    (c : X.Carrier) (z : Model n) : Model n →L[ℂ] Model n :=
  let e := extChartAt (model n) c
  (mfderiv (model n) (model n) e (e.symm z)).comp
    ((P (e.symm z)).comp (mfderiv (model n) (model n) e.symm z))

def HolomorphicProjection {X : ComplexManifold.{u} n} (P : ProjectionField X) : Prop :=
  ∀ c, DifferentiableOn ℂ (projectionInChart P c) (extChartAt (model n) c).target

def Integrable {X : ComplexManifold.{u} n} (P : ProjectionField X) : Prop :=
  ∀ c U, IsOpen U → U ⊆ (extChartAt (model n) c).target →
    ∀ V W : Model n → Model n,
      DifferentiableOn ℂ V U → DifferentiableOn ℂ W U →
      (∀ z ∈ U, projectionInChart P c z (V z) = V z) →
      (∀ z ∈ U, projectionInChart P c z (W z) = W z) →
      ∀ z ∈ U, projectionInChart P c z (VectorField.lieBracket ℂ V W z) =
        VectorField.lieBracket ℂ V W z

structure HolomorphicSplitting (X : ComplexManifold.{u} n) (r₁ r₂ : ℕ) where
  projection : ProjectionField X
  idempotent : ∀ x v, projection x (projection x v) = projection x v
  holomorphic : HolomorphicProjection projection
  first_rank : ∀ x, Module.finrank ℂ (LinearMap.range (projection x).toLinearMap) = r₁
  second_rank : ∀ x, Module.finrank ℂ (LinearMap.ker (projection x).toLinearMap) = r₂

def complementaryProjection {X : ComplexManifold.{u} n}
    (S : HolomorphicSplitting X r₁ r₂) : ProjectionField X :=
  fun x ↦ ContinuousLinearMap.id ℂ (Tangent X x) - S.projection x

def LocalBiholomorphism {X : ComplexManifold.{u} n} {Z : ComplexManifold.{v} n}
    (π : Z.Carrier → X.Carrier) : Prop :=
  ∀ z, ∃ e : OpenPartialHomeomorph Z.Carrier X.Carrier,
    z ∈ e.source ∧ (∀ w ∈ e.source, e w = π w) ∧
      MDifferentiableOn (model n) (model n) e e.source ∧
      MDifferentiableOn (model n) (model n) e.symm e.target

structure OrdinaryUniversalCover (X : ComplexManifold.{u} n)
    (Z : ComplexManifold.{v} n) where
  projection : Z.Carrier → X.Carrier
  covering : IsCoveringMap projection
  [connected : ConnectedSpace Z.Carrier]
  [simplyConnected : SimplyConnectedSpace Z.Carrier]
  liftedComplexStructure : LocalBiholomorphism projection

def CompatibleProduct {X : ComplexManifold.{u} n} {Z : ComplexManifold.{v} n}
    (S : HolomorphicSplitting X r₁ r₂) (π : OrdinaryUniversalCover X Z)
    (Y₁ : ComplexManifold.{v} r₁) (Y₂ : ComplexManifold.{v} r₂) : Prop :=
  ∃ Φ : Z.Carrier ≃ₜ (Y₁.Carrier × Y₂.Carrier),
    MDifferentiable (model n) ((model r₁).prod (model r₂)) Φ ∧
    MDifferentiable ((model r₁).prod (model r₂)) (model n) Φ.symm ∧
    (∀ z, Submodule.map
      (mfderiv (model n) ((model r₁).prod (model r₂)) Φ z).toLinearMap
      (Submodule.comap (mfderiv (model n) (model n) π.projection z).toLinearMap
        (LinearMap.range (S.projection (π.projection z)).toLinearMap)) =
      LinearMap.ker (ContinuousLinearMap.snd ℂ (Model r₁) (Model r₂)).toLinearMap) ∧
    (∀ z, Submodule.map
      (mfderiv (model n) ((model r₁).prod (model r₂)) Φ z).toLinearMap
      (Submodule.comap (mfderiv (model n) (model n) π.projection z).toLinearMap
        (LinearMap.ker (S.projection (π.projection z)).toLinearMap)) =
      LinearMap.ker (ContinuousLinearMap.fst ℂ (Model r₁) (Model r₂)).toLinearMap)

open Set
open scoped Manifold ContDiff
variable {n r₁ r₂ : ℕ}

theorem main (X : ComplexManifold.{u} n) [CompactSpace X.Carrier]
    [ConnectedSpace X.Carrier] (hn : 2 ≤ n) (hr₁ : 0 < r₁) (hr₂ : 0 < r₂)
    (g : KahlerMetric X) (S : HolomorphicSplitting X r₁ r₂)
    (h₁ : Integrable S.projection) (h₂ : Integrable (complementaryProjection S))
    (Z : ComplexManifold.{v} n) (π : OrdinaryUniversalCover X Z) :
    ∃ (Y₁ : ComplexManifold.{v} r₁) (Y₂ : ComplexManifold.{v} r₂),
      ConnectedSpace Y₁.Carrier ∧ ConnectedSpace Y₂.Carrier ∧
      SimplyConnectedSpace Y₁.Carrier ∧ SimplyConnectedSpace Y₂.Carrier ∧
      CompatibleProduct S π Y₁ Y₂ := by
  sorry

end UniversalCoverSplitting

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the two-summand form of Beauville's compatible splitting conjecture. If the tangent bundle of a compact connected Kähler manifold decomposes into two integrable holomorphic subbundles of positive rank, then its ordinary universal cover admits a product decomposition whose factor tangent bundles are the lifted specified summands.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
